Yes, we are certainly superior in our knowledge of realities, AlHamdulillah. There is no doubt about that. But that knowledge gives us no right to treat other communities or other religions with spite. It gives us no license to look down on others.

In the world today, we suffer from the disease of superiority, from the feeling that we carry some special claim over others, whether we admit it or not. It may come through religion, race, wealth, social status, or any other reason. I must not forget that we all have the same رب.

Rank can exist only in the eyes of the Master, and only according to the slave's servitude. It does not exist in the eyes of the slave.

> إِنَّ أَكْرَمَكُمْ عِندَ اللَّهِ أَتْقَاكُمْ

I stand before the same rule: the most honorable in the eyes of Allah is the one with the most piety. Nothing else gives that honor. Even the highest-ranking person is Allah's slave, and even the lowest-ranking person is Allah's slave.

This is what we recite in every أذان, five times a day:

> اشھد ان محمد عبدہ و رسولہ

I testify that Muhammad ﷺ is his **slave** and his messenger. AllahuAkbar. The highest-ranking person who ever walked this earth is the one we testify to in every prayer as the slave of Allah. Then no one has the right to claim superiority over another. If Allah has blessed a person with gifts, and the greatest gift is ایمان, faith itself, then that gift should make him more humble, not arrogant.

The Quran gives the story of one who became arrogant because of his title and rank: the story of ابلیس.

> أَنَا۠ خَيْرٌۭ مِّنْهُ . خَلَقْتَنِى مِن نَّارٍۢ وَخَلَقْتَهُۥ مِن طِينٍۢ

I am better than him. I was created from fire; he was created from clay. ابلیس was an angel, from the عالَم of angels. Adam was a human being, from the عالَم of انسان. Both were slaves of Allah. And ابلیس stood before Allah; He stood before the رَبُّ الْعَالَمِینَ and said:

I am better than him.

This story teaches a clear lesson. What did his Master do with that arrogance? Did it bring him nearer to Allah, or did it cast him out from the Divine Presence? The act for which ابلیس was punished cannot be treated lightly when it appears in me. That would not befit Allah's justice: to punish ابلیس for arrogance and remove him from His Presence, then reward the same arrogance in me by bringing me nearer to Him. Impossible.

The matter must be understood plainly. When I judge another slave and decide that one slave is superior to another, what position have I taken for myself? I have placed myself in the position of my رب.

The Quran also tells of someone who did that. Someone said, اَنَا رَبُّکُمُ الْاَعْلَی. I am your greatest رب. فرعون said that. His end is known.

The reality is that فرعون knew he was human. He knew he was not a رب. He knew his own weakness. Yet he still claimed a station he did not deserve. He even hated the fact that he needed to go to the toilet, because that was not fitting, in his mind, for a رب.

So he gathered all his physicians and asked them how he could escape this problem. They told him to eat bananas. He ate bananas for forty days, and then he ended up with a much bigger problem.

I keep repeating and stressing this because it is no longer only a Fironic problem. It has become a global problem. Everyone wants to become الہ; everyone wants to become معبود. At some level, we have all become people who want to be worshiped. Yet we forget how Allah defines us in the Quran:

> وَلَقَدْ خَلَقْنَا ٱلْإِنسَنَ مِن سُلَلَةٍۢ مِّن طِينٍۢ

Human beings come from a chain of dirt, from mud and water mixed together.

> ثُمَّ جَعَلَ نَسْلَهُۥ مِن سُلَلَةٍۢ مِّن مَّآءٍۢ مَّهِينٍۢ

I understand Allah telling these people: “We created your progeny from a lowly fluid. Who do you think you are? Get down off your high horse. Be a رجل.”

A رجل is the one who has ِرَجَلَ. As it is said: ِرَجَلَ عن فَرِسِہ. He is the one who comes down from his high horse and plants his feet on the ground. He does not see himself as raised above the earth. He returns to the level from which he came: dust. That is الرجل.

Yet I see that all of us, at some level, are playing games. We are trying to be الہ. Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى says no: “Be a رجل. Be بنی آدم. Be آدمی.” That is what it truly means to accept Allah as رَبُّ الْعَالَمِینَ. Humility, not arrogance, is the mark of a believer.

The acceptance of our عبودیۃ, our slavery and servitude to Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى, is a heavy burden that I, as a believer, carry on my back. When a burden rests on the back of a person climbing a mountain, he bends forward; he does not walk upright. The burden makes him bend. It makes him humble. This is why humility is the essence of spiritual progress. Maulana Ali says

> مَنْ تَوَاضَعَ لِی اللَّہُ، رَفَعَ اللَّہُ لَہُ

Whoever humbles himself for Allah, Allah raises him and lifts him in rank. For this reason, the highest-ranking person ever to walk the face of the earth was my beloved Prophet مُحَمَّدٌ مُصْطَفَی ﷺ. The greatest man this world will ever know was also the humblest in character. Indeed, humility was his distinguishing mark. The earlier prophecies that foretold his coming identified him by the greatness of his humility. SubhanAllah. The greatest mark of his prophethood was his humility.

> یُریدُ أَنَّ یَبعَثُ أُمِیِّ مِنَ الأُمِّیِّن۔وأجعل السكينةَ لباسُهُ ، والبِرَّ شَعارُهُ ، والتقوى ضَميرَهُ ، والحِكمَةَ مَنطِقَهُ ، والصِّدقَ والوفاءَ طَبِيعَتُهُ ، والعَفوَ وَالمَعرُوفَ خُلقُهُ ، والَحقَّ شَريعَتَهُ ، والعَدلَ سيرَتَهُ ، والهَدِىُ إمامُهُ ، والإسلامَ مِلَّتُهُ ، وأحمد أِسمُهُ .

> أَهدِي بِهِ بَعدَ الضَّلالَة ، وأعلَمُ بِهِ مِنَ الجَهالة ، وأَرفَعُ به بعد الخَمالَة ، وأعرِفُ به بعد النَّكِرَة ، وأكثَرُ بِهِ بعدَ القِلَّة ، وأغنِي به بعد العَيلَة ، وأَجمِعُ به بعد الفُرقَة

I recognize here the prediction, in the Bible, of the arrival of Rasul Allah ﷺ. Allah mentions these beautiful signs of his coming:

I am going to send you a man from your own nation. His garment will be a peaceful temperament; his behavior will be kind; his conscience will be pious; his reasoning will be wise. Honesty and truth will be his nature; forgiveness will fill his dealings; justice will mark his actions; Ahmed will be his name.

> أَهدِي بِهِ بَعدَ الضَّلالَة ، وأعلَمُ بِهِ مِنَ الجَهالة ، وأَرفَعُ به بعد الخَمالَة ، وأعرِفُ به بعد النَّكِرَة ، وأكثَرُ بِهِ بعدَ القِلَّة ، وأغنِي به بعد العَيلَة

I will guide a nation through him and bring them out of error. I will give them knowledge through him and bring them out of ignorance. I will raise him after he had been belittled, make him known after he had been unknown, increase him after scarcity, and enrich him after poverty. Allah continues to mention these qualities of His beloved. Then, after all these beautiful traits and astonishing signs, He mentions the greatest sign of all.

> لَو یَمشِی عَلی القَصَبِ الیَابِس۔۔۔ لَم یُسمَع مِن تَحتِ قَدَمَیہِ مِن سَکِینَتِہِ

He will be deeply humble. He will walk among people with such humility that, when he steps on dry leaves, the crackle beneath his feet will not be heard. SubhanAllah. This is the Prophet I follow. This is the example I follow.

The honor this slave of Allah received from his Master came through one quality: humility. That is what separates the true believer from the hypocrite.

For this reason, I see atheism as a blatant form of arrogance. The atheist rejects the most obvious and most basic fact of his own existence: he was **created**. Even he cannot truly say that he was not created. If I say that my father created me, and his father created him before him, that is what they call دور; that is تسلسل. The chain keeps going backward. Where does it stop? It stops at Adam. Then who created Adam? That is where the matter ends.

> وکُلُّکُم مِن آدَم. وَ آدَم مِن تُرَاب

All of you are from Adam, and Adam is from dust. Arrogance does not befit dirt, because dirt is constantly trampled underfoot.

Maulana Ali saw a man acting arrogantly, and he said to him, “Who do you think you are? Your beginning is a vile, dirty fluid. Your end is a disgusting, decaying, foul-smelling corpse. Between those two stages, you walk through life carrying two loathsome things inside your body: your urine and your feces. Your creation comes from the union of two ugly body parts. What right do you have to act arrogantly before Allah and his creation?”

This is the first matter: I must know my station in this world, and I must know who I am. I am Marboob. I am created. I will die. I will be raised up. This understanding, that I am Marboob, is the station of the slave. It is the knowledge that I am not Mabood. I am not the creator.

Wallahi, I give myself sincere advice: humility belongs in every walk of life. Arrogance must not take root. It is the greatest challenge of our time. Arrogance is the أذان; it is the call of our age. It has become the trademark of worldly success: in schools, in colleges, at work, in families, and in communities. The whole claim is, “I am better than you.” Arrogance does not befit an obedient slave.

May Allah grant us all humility, guide us, and enable us to walk in the footsteps of our beloved prophet ﷺ.

If I grasp even this much, if I truly understand the meaning of Rabb Alalameen, the world becomes a beautiful place.

If I understand that Allah is the Rabb Alalameen, then I understand that He is the One who guides everyone. He is the King. He is the Owner. He is the One who cares, the One who guides, and the One who gives gifts. Then everything in my life is between me and Him.

He will take care of me. I do not need to worry about anything. I do not need to judge another slave. I do not need to hurt another slave. I do not need to show my authority or power over another slave, even if I am hurt, even if I am wronged, especially if I have been wronged.

My Master will take care of me. My Master will come to my rescue. He will come to my defense on the Day of Judgment, and He will make that wrong right. He will stand behind me and support me. I do not need to worry about anything.

Whether I believe it or not, whether I want it or not, that Day is coming: the Day when the Rabb will gather everyone and exact justice.

> رَبَّنَآ إِنَّكَ جَامِعُ ٱلنَّاسِ لِيَوْمٍۢ لَّا رَيْبَ فِيهِ ۚ

Oh our Master. Indeed, You will gather all of us on a Day about which there is absolutely no doubt, because You have promised us that.

> إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَا يُخْلِفُ ٱلْمِيعَادَ

There is no doubt. Allah never goes against His promise. I remind myself of this every time I recite the Fateha. There is a reason I pray fifty-one rakaats in one day, and all fifty-one times I recite Fateha: so that every time I say Rabb Alalameen, I remind myself of this hierarchy.

I understand who He is, and who I am in this relationship.

> وآخر الدعوانا عن الحمد للہ رَبُّ الْعَالَمِینَ

A man gave Rasul Allah a bottle of wine as a gift.

I think of the man who traveled a long distance to meet Rasul Allah (Salawaat). When he reached him, he brought the Messenger a bottle of wine as a gift. He said, “Ya RasulAllah, I got this special wine as a gift for you.”

I know how such a scene often unfolds. A very secularized person arrives at a Muslim home with a bottle of wine as a housewarming gift, and the reaction becomes outrage: “La hawla wala quwwata. How dare you? Do you not know I am a Muslim? Do you not know this is haraam? Get the hell out of my house. Never show me your face again.”

But this man brought the bottle to Rasul Allah, the most pious man I can imagine. He smiled. He smiled and said, “Brother, did you not hear Allah's command that alcohol has been prohibited?”

He said, “No, Rasul Allah. I never heard that!”

He immediately gave the bottle to his slave and whispered something in his ear. Rasul Allah says, “What did you just say to him?”

The man said, “I told him to sell the bottle in the market.”

Rasul Allah smiled again and said, “The One who has prohibited the consumption of intoxicants has also forbidden its trade.”

So the man told the slave to pour it out.

This is the character of Rasul Allah. This is his humility. This is the Tarbiyyah, his rububiyyah. SubhanAllah. He did not say, “Do you not know who I am? How dare you bring this filth into my presence?” He never did that.

Hearts are never conquered with harshness. Hearts have always been conquered only with love and kindness. I need to study and learn what kind of Rabb, what kind of parent, and what kind of teacher our Prophet ﷺ was. SubhanAllah.

## The Divine Formula For Recognizing Allah

I examined the term عالَم. It means anything and everything that the عالِم can know, so that he may recognize the علیم.

Knowledge needs an object before it can be gained. To learn human physiology, I need the human body. To learn mathematics, I need numbers. To study physics, I need physical things to exist. As human beings, I cannot know a subject unless there is an object for that knowledge. This عالَم, then, is the object through which I gain knowledge of the علیم.

I also examined the statement رَبُّ الْعَالَمِینَ. It shows that Allah is not only the master of this universe, but the master of everything the human intellect can know. Everything in creation therefore reflects the علیم. Whether it is physical, metaphysical, conceptual, or imaginable, whatever it may be, it reflects the علیم. The fact that he created this infinite universe for us reflects not only his glory and magnificence, but also his generosity and the vastness of his رحمۃ.

### Divine Formula

To place this in perspective, two fundamental questions stand around the understanding of God. The first is: Who is God? The second is: What does he want from us? These are the two essential questions mankind has grappled with from the beginning of time: Who is God, and what does he want from us?

I take the first question, “Who is God?” to include further questions: What is He capable of? What are His attributes? What is His personality? What are His traits?

I take the second question, “What does He want from us?” to include questions such as: Why did He create this universe? What is our purpose on this earth? What does He expect from us?

Allah answers both questions through a very simple and straightforward formula in a verse of the Quran. He says:

Question

![](images/81/cd8a1733-7cb4-4e8a-a67a-cbd7e47ed8f0.jpg)

In this verse, He mentions two broad categories: the horizons and the soul. The horizons are everything outside us: the physical universe. The soul is everything within us: our spiritual universe.

So how does Allah answer the question, “Who am I?”

Answers

![](images/81/b813ade8-e959-40c9-8d6a-4fc1a4737099.jpg)

He answers it through the horizons: the physical universe and everything within it.

How does He answer the question, “What do I want from you?” He answers it through the نفس: through our own creation and existence, and through the creation of our spirit and soul.

This is what I call the genius of Allah's design. I find it astonishing that He answers such a complex and seemingly impossible question with such a simple formula. In my personal view, this is my absolute proof of the existence of God and of the Quran as the message of Allah, because this kind of perfection in design cannot exist at the human level.

This is also Allah's solution to the problem that theology faces in modern times: the question of God's existence. The problem today is that I try to invent and apply human formulas to prove that Allah exists. Yet Allah has already given His own formula in the Quran. It is simple, and it leads to the conclusion because it is the only formula that can work.

If I try to use my own formulas, they fail, because this universe is His kingdom and His design. His rules govern it. The world gives many examples of this. An iPhone works according to Apple's specifications; used outside those specifications, it does not work as intended. In the same way, Allah's formula for recognizing Him is the آفاق and the أنفس.

Each part of the formula has to be understood in its place.

### Horizons

The first part of the formula is the آفاق, the horizons. This part answers several questions:

- Who is Allah?
- What is He capable of?
- Why is He worthy of my worship, my willing slavery, and my servitude?
- What makes Him so glorious, magnificent, and overwhelming in my sight that I would even consider Him my Master?

Allah answers these questions through the signs in the آفاق, His signs in the horizons, meaning His signs in this universe. This subject belongs to the conclusions of reflection. There, I examined the prerequisite for understanding this formula. That prerequisite is this:

1. I must have a sound intellect and a pure heart. The Quran calls this لُب, the core. It also describes such people as اُولُو الْاَلْبَابِ: people whose hearts are pure and whose intellects are sound. Only the two together allow me to see reality as it truly is.

1. The second requirement in the Quran, and in this formula, is an uncontaminated and pure فطرۃ. It grows from a pure heart and a sound intellect.

The instrument by which I study the universe and learn from it is the intellect, العقل. Its work is to define things and set their limits. The intellect is the container, and knowledge is the content placed inside it. What, then, is knowledge (عِلْم)? In simple terms, knowledge is understanding and recognizing the true reality of things.

Concept and judgment

![](images/81/5f48abc4-58c6-4794-9d67-7a23731cf232.jpg)

That understanding begins with a concept, called تصور in Arabic. First, the reality is formed in the mind. Then comes حکم: a ruling or judgment about the truth of that reality. The Arabs say:

> الحُکمُ علی شَیء فرحٌ عَن تَصَوُّرِہِ

Before I can judge a thing, I must define it and understand it in every dimension that can be grasped. Once I have formed a clear concept of that reality, I can judge whether it is true or false, good or bad, and so on.

Propositions

![](images/81/04fc84c0-c8c1-4ef9-a632-89eb104a4873.jpg)

I ask first how I, as a human being, form a concept of things. Scholars say that propositions are of three kinds.

A proposition is a statement or assertion that expresses a judgment or an opinion. For example, I may state, "fire burns," or "fire is bright." That statement is then tested against observation, and from that observation I judge whether it is true or false. This is how a proposition works.

Scholars divide propositions into three kinds:

- Empirical propositions
- Rational propositions
- Philosophical or revelatory propositions

### Empirical Propositions

The first kind is empirical propositions. These rest on observation or experience through the five senses: sight, hearing, smell, taste, and touch. They are also called sensory propositions. They belong to the realm that can be witnessed, which the Quran calls عَالَمُ الشَّھَادَۃِ.

So when I say that fire is hot, that it is bright, or that combustion takes place when it combines with oxygen, I am making empirical or sensory propositions. Their truth can be observed through the five senses.

### Rational Propositions

The second kind is the rational proposition. It is a logical proposition, such as: 3 + 2 equals 5; therefore, 5 - 3 must equal 2. This kind of proposition works through inference.

If I see smoke rising in the distance, I can infer that there is a fire there, even though I cannot see the fire itself. That inference rests on earlier empirical knowledge: when something burns, it gives off smoke. From that known fact, I can reason that a fire must be present.

Yet if smoke is the only evidence I consider, my inference may be wrong. The smoke might be coming from a large group of people having a cigar party. To preserve the rational proposition that there is a fire, I need more empirical evidence. If I walk toward the smoke and see the area becoming brighter and feel the air becoming warmer, then the likelihood of my rational proposition increases. I now have more sensory data, in the form of light and heat, supporting the claim that there is a fire.

In other words, the more empirical evidence I have, the stronger the rational proposition becomes.

That is how Sherlock Holmes works. He uses empirical data to deduce rational propositions.

### Philosophical / Revelatory Propositions

I now turn to the third type of proposition: the philosophical or revelatory proposition. These are the Quranic propositions that speak about عَالَمُ الْغَیْبِ, the Unseen Realm. They speak of the existence of a Creator, angels, divine books, messengers, the Day of Judgment, heaven, and hell.

This is, in broad terms, what the Quran does. It gives philosophical or revelatory propositions. It sets before me the reality of the afterlife, the Day of Judgment, heaven, and hell. These are the realities in which I place my faith. My ایمان rests on these revelatory propositions.

The question is how I arrive there. How can I believe in propositions that are wholly unseen and metaphysical? The clearest way to disclose the hidden realm to intelligent human beings is to fashion the physical universe on the same principles as the spiritual universe. Then everything in the physical universe points back to the workings of the spiritual universe.

An object that points toward a destination is called a sign. So Allah says:

> سَنُرِيهِمْ آيَتِنَا فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ

He will show them His signs, His evidence. Where? فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ, in the horizons. What appears in the horizons is empirical evidence. Everything outside me exists as observable data. The physical things around me are things I can see, hear, touch, taste, and smell. وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ And within their own selves. As rational human beings, I can deduce and infer rational propositions from empirical data. Therefore:

> سَنُرِيهِمْ آيَتِنَا فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ -

He will show them the evidence of the unseen realm through the empirical and rational data of this universe. How much evidence?

> حَتَّىٰ يَتَبَيَّنَ لَهُمْ أَنَّهُ ٱلْحَقُّ

Unlimited evidence, until it becomes absolutely clear to them that these revelatory propositions are true.

For example, the Quran tells me that Allah is the creator of this universe. The fact that the universe is a created thing is supported by rational and empirical observation. I know that there was a time in the past when this universe did not exist. I also know that a time will come in the future when it will return to non-existence. Existence and non-existence are both attributes of creation. Therefore, by reason, this universe is a creation.

Empirical data also confirm that every creation requires a creator. If this universe is a creation, it must have a creator. So when the Quran proposes:

> اللہ الذی خلق السماوت و الارض

Allah is the one who created the heavens and the earth. That revelatory proposition is then backed and supported by rational and empirical evidence in the horizons, evidence that the human mind can understand and process.

Once I can believe this particular proposition rationally, it becomes easier to believe Allah when He says that He also created a spiritual universe. Logically, the One who has the power to create this physical universe must also have the power to create the spiritual universe.

When I understand this phenomenon, I can appreciate the motive of the Quran. This is why, throughout the Quran, Allah asks me to reflect on the creations of the heavens and the earth. He never asks me to reflect on Allah Himself, because that would be impossible without the empirical and rational data of this physical world. That is why He says:

> إِنَّ فِى خَلْقِ ٱلسَّمَوَتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَٱخْتِلَفِ ٱلَّيْلِ وَٱلنَّهَارِ لَآيَتٍۢ لِّأُو۟لِى ٱلْأَلْبَبِ

Without doubt, in the **creation** of the heavens and the earth, and in the alternation of night and day, there are signs for people of intellect.

The word لُب carries a subtle meaning here. It points to the pith or seed, something hidden beneath the earth. In the same way, my intellect becomes the means by which I understand what is unseen through what is seen. From the plant before me, I can infer the hidden seed and the hidden roots. I do not need to uproot the plant and dig into the soil before I can reach the rational conclusion that roots must exist.

This is why Allah calls me to reflect on His creations: so that I may gather the empirical and rational evidence He has placed before my intellect. That evidence makes it easier to accept the truth disclosed by revelation.

This is why Maulana Ali taught:

> لَا تَفَکَّرْ فِی اللہِ ..... وَلَاکِنْ تَفَکَّرْ فِی خَلْقِ اللَّہِ

Do not reflect upon Allah; instead, reflect upon the “creations” of Allah. In witnessing this universe lies the evidence for what exists beyond it. The more I reflect on this physical creation, the more it answers my questions about the spiritual creation, and especially about its creator.

So the Quran proposes:

> أَنَا اللَّـهُ الْعَزِيزُ الْحَكِيمُ

Indeed I am Allah: the One whose power is complete and whose wisdom is perfect. The universe offers empirical and rational evidence for this claim. Its study shows that it could only have been created by One of immense power and immense wisdom, because every created thing exists with purpose. Human creation, therefore, must also have a purpose.

The Quran proposes:

> ٱلرَّحْمَنُ ‎۱‏عَلَّمَ ٱلْقُرْآنَ ‎۲‏خَلَقَ ٱلْإِنسَنَ ‎۳‏عَلَّمَهُ ٱلْبَيَانَ

The Intensely Merciful taught the Quran, created man, and taught him speech. A careful study of these three acts shows what they disclose:

- the teaching of the Quran,
- the creation of mankind,
- the granting of speech.

Together, they clearly prove that this could only be the act of a ٱلرَّحْمَنُ. The Quran proposes:

> اللَّهُ لا إِلَهَ إِلا هُوَ الْحَيُّ الْقَيُّومُ

Allah is the One besides whom there is no deity worthy of worship. He is the Eternally Living and the ultimate maintainer of all things. Empirical and rational evidence shows that life is necessary for maintenance. All life on this earth is temporary; every created being will inevitably die. Whoever dies cannot remain the maintainer, sustainer, or provider for those who depend on him. Therefore, only the Ever-Living can be the ultimate maintainer and sustainer. This supports the next revelatory proposition:

> لا تَأْخُذُهُ سِنَةٌ وَلا نَوْمٌ

He is never overcome by drowsiness, and He never sleeps. I know that drowsiness weakens mental ability and focus, while sleep removes them altogether for a time. A person who is drowsy, unconscious, or asleep cannot be all-mighty, nor can he be the ultimate maintainer and sustainer. Constant maintenance requires complete attention and vigilance.

The remaining propositions unfold from this:

> لَهُ مَا فِي السَّمَاوَاتِ وَمَا فِي الأَرْضِ

Everything in the heavens and the earth belongs to Him.

> مَنْ ذَا الَّذِي يَشْفَعُ عِنْدَهُ إِلا بِإِذْنِهِ

Who can intercede on anyone's behalf without His permission?

> يَعْلَمُ مَا بَيْنَ أَيْدِيهِمْ وَمَا خَلْفَهُمْ وَلا يُحِيطُونَ بِشَيْءٍ مِنْ عِلْمِهِ إِلا بِمَا شَاءَ

He knows fully what lies ahead and what has already come to pass. No one shares in that knowledge except those whom He chooses to grant a share of it.

> وَسِعَ كُرْسِيُّهُ السَّمَاوَاتِ وَالأَرْضَ وَلا يَئُودُهُ حِفْظُهُمَا

His throne extends over the heavens and the earth. He maintains control over the universe, and that maintenance neither tires Him nor wearies Him.

> وَهُوَ الْعَلِيُّ الْعَظِيمُ

I recognize Him as the All-Mighty and the All-Exalted.

When I study these revealed statements alongside the evidence that the universe itself gives through observation and reason, the first statement becomes easy to affirm: اللَّهُ لا إِلَهَ إِلا هُوَ Allah alone is the deity worthy of my worship. No created thing in this universe, living or lifeless, bears that exalted description. If I am to choose any being for worship, reason itself points me to Allah alone.

This is the first part of the formula: the design of the horizons, the آفاق. The horizons give observable and rational evidence for the revealed propositions of the Quran. Through them, I am given a path toward knowing my Creator and recognizing the magnificent powers He possesses, because the effects of His divine attributes appear throughout His creation. This is the story of the Martian and the iPad from the discussion on Conclusions of Reflection.

### Self/Soul

The second part of the formula is the أنفس, the soul or the self. The soul answers a different question: what Allah wants from us. That becomes clear when I return to the moment when Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى decided to create the first soul, Adam AS. How did He describe His intention to the angels? He said:

> وَإِذْ قَالَ رَبُّكَ لِلْمَلَٓئِكَةِ إِنِّى جَاعِلٌۭ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ خَلِيفَةًۭ ۖ قَالُوٓا۟ أَتَجْعَلُ فِيهَا مَن يُفْسِدُ فِيهَا وَيَسْفِكُ ٱلدِّمَآءَ وَنَحْنُ نُسَبِّحُ بِحَمْدِكَ وَنُقَدِّسُ لَكَ ۖ قَالَ إِنِّىٓ أَعْلَمُ مَا لَا تَعْلَمُونَ
>
> And when your Lord said to the angels: "Verily, I am going to place generations after generations on earth." They said: "Will You place therein those who will make mischief therein and shed blood, - while we glorify You with praises and thanks and sanctify You." He said: "I know that which you do not know."

I begin with His words to the angels: “I am going to create my caliph, my خلیفۃ, on this earth.” I understand this word, خلیفۃ, through its root. The word خلیفۃ comes from خَلَفَ, which means “back” or “behind.” Its opposite is سَلَفَ, which means “the front” or “what lies ahead.” So خلف means the one who comes behind, the one who follows, or the one who succeeds another. In this sense, خلیفۃ means “representative.” Allah said to the angels, “I am planning to create my representative on earth.”

Caliph

![](images/81/9ce920c6-9fdc-45d1-a2a2-85e8cc4c7774.jpg)

A representative is someone chosen or appointed to act or speak on behalf of another. If I were to choose someone to represent me accurately, I would look for someone who shared my traits, or at least carried traits close enough to mine to make true representation possible. Without that likeness, the person could not represent me or convey my thoughts faithfully.

For this reason, in the story of Adam's creation, Allah did not only create Adam AS. He also taught him all His traits.

> وَعَلَّمَ آدَمَ ٱلْأَسْمَآءَ كُلَّهَا ثُمَّ عَرَضَهُمْ عَلَى ٱلْمَلَٓئِكَةِ فَقَالَ أَنۢبِـُٔونِى بِأَسْمَآءِ هَٓؤُلَآءِ إِن كُنتُمْ صَدِقِينَ
>
> And He taught Adam all the names , then He showed them to the angels and said, "Tell Me the names of these if you are truthful."

He taught Adam all the names. He taught Adam all the divine traits. In other words, He trained Adam AS in the divine traits that Allah Himself possessed. Then He presented Adam AS to the angels and challenged them through those traits. What did the angels say?

> قَالُوا۟ سُبْحَنَكَ لَا عِلْمَ لَنَآ إِلَّا مَا عَلَّمْتَنَآ ۖ إِنَّكَ أَنتَ ٱلْعَلِيمُ ٱلْحَكِيمُ

The angels said: "May You be glorified. We know nothing except what You have taught us. We have not received the training Adam AS received. Therefore, we admit that we cannot be Your true representatives on earth." That had been their original argument.

> وَإِذْ قَالَ رَبُّكَ لِلْمَلَٓئِكَةِ إِنِّى جَاعِلٌۭ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ خَلِيفَةًۭ ۖ قَالُوٓا۟ أَتَجْعَلُ فِيهَا مَن يُفْسِدُ فِيهَا وَيَسْفِكُ ٱلدِّمَآءَ وَنَحْنُ نُسَبِّحُ بِحَمْدِكَ وَنُقَدِّسُ لَكَ ۖ قَالَ إِنِّىٓ أَعْلَمُ مَا لَا تَعْلَمُونَ

I understand the angels' question as an objection to Adam's fitness for this trust: will this representative spill blood and wreak havoc, while they themselves are constantly praising and glorifying Allah? They saw themselves as able to represent Him on earth. They could be His true خلیفۃ, and so the need for Adam was not yet clear to them.

But when Allah created Adam and trained him to reflect the divine attributes in his character, they all accepted what had been shown to them. In that sense, they validated and confirmed that Adam, and through him mankind, alone possesses the true capacity to represent Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى on the earth.

This is the meaning of Allah creating man in his own image. We possess the attributes that our creator and master possesses. I know that Allah sees. He sees our actions and our deeds:

> وَٱللَّهُ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ بَصِيرٌۭ

I know that Allah hears:

> وَهُوَ ٱلسَّمِيعُ ٱلْعَلِيمُ

For this reason, he gave us the ability to hear, so that we could represent his qualities accurately on this earth. More than that, he gave us hearing so that we could understand what it means when he says that he watches our actions and hears our cries and supplications.

I know that Allah acts, because we have the ability to act:

> كُلَّ يَوْمٍ هُوَ فِى شَأْنٍۢ

Every single day, he is busy in work. I know that Allah has the ability to love, because the Quran says that he loves everything good, clean, and pure:

> إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يُحِبُّ ٱلتَّوَّبِينَ وَيُحِبُّ ٱلْمُتَطَهِّرِينَ

For this reason, he gave us the ability to love. That is why it belongs to our فطرۃ, our natural predisposition, to love what is clean and pure: because that is what our creator loves.

Similarly, I also know that Allah has the ability to hate and dislike.

> وَٱللَّهُ لَا يُحِبُّ ٱلْفَسَادَ

I know that Allah hates and dislikes corruption. لَا وَٱللَّهُ يُحِبُّ. For that reason, he placed in our innate nature a dislike for filth and foulness. We recoil from what is dirty because corruption is hateful to him.

This is because we have been created in God's image. We have been created with the same divine attributes that Allah possesses, in a created and limited way, so that we may serve as his true representatives, his true خلیفۃ, on this earth. Through these qualities, I come to know and understand my creator.

The way is simple: I look into my own self.

> وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِكُمْ ۚ أَفَلَا تُبْصِرُونَ

The verse calls the human being back to reflection on the self. What I seek for myself points toward what Allah wants from me. SubhanAllah. I want justice; Allah wants justice. I want to be loved; Allah wants me to love him. I like to be praised; Allah likes to be praised. I appreciate gratitude; Allah likes his creation to be grateful to him. I feel anger when people violate what belongs to me or ignore my rules; Allah is angered when his possessions and his rules are violated. I want others to give me due priority in their lives; Allah wants me to make him the priority in mine.

I can read the whole Quran from cover to cover and find no demand Allah makes of me that I would not, in some form, expect from someone else. If I want to know what Allah wants from me, I need only reflect on my own self. What do I want for myself from others? That reveals the matter.

I want kind treatment, and Allah wants kind treatment. I want to be treated as though I matter, and Allah wants me to treat Him as though He matters. I want credit for what I make, and Allah wants credit for what He made: for creating me, and for creating this universe for me. SubhanAllah!

The question of who created the universe returns first to the self. I was created in His image. I was created with abilities that reflect, in a creaturely way, abilities Allah possesses. He is خالق. He is the Creator, and He gave me the ability to create and construct. He made me خالق. Even a needle cannot exist in my world unless someone makes it. How, then, can a human being created in His image think that this universe came into existence without a creator?

I hear complaints about the state of this world. I hear the question of why I do not do something about it. I already did: I created the human being. I created the human being in My own image. I gave the human being My powers.

The answer, then, turns back upon the one who complains. Poverty and misery are not only matters to lament. I gave wealth so that it could be spent to make life better and easier for others. Injustice is not only a subject to speak about. I gave courage so that injustices could be corrected. Blind people are not only to be seen; a person can become their sight. Weak people are not only to be pitied; a person can become their strength.

I gave everything needed to repair the conditions of this world. Yet human beings often do not want to leave their comfort zones. They want only to complain and to have others solve their problems for them. That is not how it will work, because I created them as gods. I created them as الہ. Each and every one of them has been created with the capabilities of an الہ, so that they can recognize and identify the ال الہ, and so that they can understand what their اللہ wants from them. SubhanAllah.

If the question is what I want from the human being, the answer lies within the self. By studying one's own capabilities and desires, a person comes to know what I want.

> مَنْ عَرَفَ نَفْسَہُ فَقَدْ عَرَفَ رَبَّہُ

The one who comes to know his own self comes to know his master, because the slave has been created in the image of his master.

### Rights Of Allah

This opens a wide field of reflection. The matter is too large for me to cover within these sessions, and each soul must reflect on it and seek the answers that belong to its own condition. One example, however, belongs here: the question of rights, حقوق.

Human beings have rights. They are entitled to basic rights. From what has already been established, I must also recognize that Allah has rights. Just as there are human rights, there are divine rights. Human rights rest on divine rights; divine rights do not rest on human rights. If human rights carry weight, then divine rights carry far greater weight.

Among human rights is the right to be treated with honor. After all that a parent does for her child, and after all that a mother endures to bring her child into this world, she gains a right of gratitude over that child. After the hard work a sincere and dedicated teacher gives to her students, she gains a right of gratitude over them: a right to respect, dignity, and honor.

If created beings can understand and value the rights that human beings hold over one another, then what is the right of the One who created them all from nothing?

> وَاللَّـهُ أَخْرَجَكُم مِّن بُطُونِ أُمَّهَاتِكُمْ لَا تَعْلَمُونَ شَيْئًا

What is the right of Allah, who brought the human being out from the wombs of mothers in a state of complete ignorance?

> وَجَعَلَ لَكُمُ السَّمْعَ وَالْأَبْصَارَ وَالْأَفْئِدَةَۙ

What, then, is the right of Allah, who gave the human being hearing, sight, and a beating heart?

> لَعَلَّكُمْ تَشْكُرُونَ

So that gratitude might arise.

When one human being provides for another, the least expected in return is gratitude. A fair exchange would be the same generosity, or something close to it. If that is not given, gratitude remains the minimum due. What, then, of Allah?

> لَا نَسْأَلُكَ رِزْقًاۖ نَّحْنُ نَرْزُقُكَۗوَ

We do not ask you for provision; indeed, it is We who provide for you. What, then, is the right of gratitude owed to the One who takes nothing and gives everything?

> وَمَا بِكُم مِّن نِّعْمَةٍ فَمِنَ اللَّـهِۖثُمَّ

Every good thing in a person's possession has been given by Allah.

When human beings make something for a purpose, or invent something for a purpose, they claim the right to require that it be used properly. They expect it to serve the purpose for which it was made. If it is misused, they feel entitled to be upset. They feel entitled to demand justice, and even to bring the offender to court.

What, then, of the purpose for which Allah created?

> وَمَا خَلَقْتُ الْجِنَّ وَالْإِنسَ إِلَّا لِيَعْبُدُونِ

I have not created jinn and mankind except to worship Me. How, then, am I fulfilling Allah's right? Does He not have the right to be displeased and angry when I fail to fulfill those rights?

I must think deeply about the divine formula that Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى has given through this verse:

> سَنُرِيهِمْ آيَتِنَا فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ

He will show His signs in the horizons and within the self, so that the human being may come to recognize who Allah is and what He wants from him. May Allah bless us all with the wisdom to fulfill His rights and obligations, and enable us to treat Him with the honor and dignity He deserves.

If anyone in this world treated me the way I treat Allah, I would be deeply offended. I have been created in the image of the One who possesses the ultimate rights. That is all I truly am: an image, a reflection of a distorted version of the divine attributes. I am not real in myself. I am an illusion, like a reflection in a mirror, present for a moment and gone the next. But Allah is real. His rights are real. His obligations are real.

I will end with a beautiful story I heard recently.

A man once heard about a saint known for deep learning. He decided to travel to him and ask for advice. After a long journey, he finally reached the saint, greeted him, and said: I have heard that you are a great scholar and a ولی, a friend of Allah. Please give me some advice!

The saint said to him: My advice is this: “If you do not consider Allah your superior, then at least consider Him your equal.”

When the man heard this advice, he said to himself, “What kind of foolish advice is this? Who would be so foolish as not to consider Allah his equal? Is this what I traveled all this way for? I have wasted my whole journey visiting this so-called saint.”

He was thinking all this, and the saint was reading his thoughts.

The saint said to him: You must be tired after your long journey. Spend the night in this inn, and leave in the morning.

The man agreed and spent the night there.

The next morning, after he woke and offered his morning prayer, a servant came to his door carrying a large tray. On one side of the tray was a fresh breakfast for the man:

- 4 eggs
- 4 pieces of buttered toast
- 2 pieces of chicken
- 2 glasses of orange juice

On the other side was yesterday's food: some stale bread and lentils.

Just as the man was about to eat, a beggar came to the door and called out to him. He said, “In the name of your creator I ask you for help.

“I have not eaten for two days! For the love of your master, please give me something to eat.” So the man gave the stale bread and lentils to the beggar, and he ate all the fresh food himself.

After breakfast, he prepared to leave and decided to visit the saint so he could bid him farewell. He found the saint and said his goodbyes. Just as he was about to go, the saint says to him: “My son! Remember my advice! If you do not consider Allah your superior, then at least consider Him your equal.”

The man lost his temper and yelled at the saint: “What kind of foolish advice is this? Who in this world would be so senseless as not to consider Allah his superior? Even a child knows something so simple, and you are giving me such absurd advice?”

The saint became furious at the man. He says: “Listen! This morning, when you sat down for breakfast, there was fresh food and stale food on your plate. A beggar came to you and asked you for food in the name of your Creator. He asked you for food for the love of your Master.

“Had you considered Allah superior to yourself, you would have eaten the stale bread and lentils and given the fresh food to the beggar. Had you considered Allah your equal, you would have shared your fresh food with him. You would have eaten two eggs and given two eggs to him. You would have eaten two pieces of toast and given two pieces to him. You would have enjoyed one glass of orange juice and let the beggar enjoy the other. O wretched creation! Forget superior; you did not even consider Allah your equal.”

I end with the same advice to myself: “If we do not consider Allah our superior, then, in view of His rights over us and all His favors upon us, let us at least consider Him our equal.”

## The Horizons And The Soul

I have considered the Quranic formula that gives me an understanding of who our Creator is and what his intentions are. That formula is declared in the verse:

Formula

![](images/86/718f2946-a675-46a9-ae87-0aaea1e7e6f8.jpg)

> سَنُرِيهِمْ آيَتِنَا فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ حَتَّىٰ يَتَبَيَّنَ لَهُمْ أَنَّهُ ٱلْحَقُّ ۗ أَوَلَمْ يَكْفِ بِرَبِّكَ أَنَّهُۥ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَىْءٍۢ شَهِيدٌ

We will show them Our Signs in the universe, and in their own selves, until it becomes absolutely clear to them that this is the truth.

The signs in the horizons disclose the nature and power of Allah. The signs within our souls disclose the intentions of Allah, because Allah created us all in his own image, and he demands for himself only what we demand from others as rights.

In these analogies between the ظاہر and the باطن — the horizons and the soul — I find insight into the inner workings of دین. Rasul Allah ﷺ said in a HADEES:

> اِنَّا اللہ اَسَّسَ دِینَہُ عَلیَ مِثاَلِ خَلقِہِ لِیَستَدِلَّ بِالدِّینِ عَلَی الدُّنیَا ؛ وَ الدُّنیَا عَلَی الدِّین

I understand from this that Allah founded his religion on the pattern of creation. He made the world a proof for his religion, and he made his religion a proof for the world. The physical world and the spiritual world therefore rest on the same principles and patterns. Through the physical world, the human intellect can grasp signs that lead it toward the spiritual world. The horizons and the soul, or the self, are therefore keys by which I learn about Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى.

### Horizon - Refraction Of Attributes

He scattered the knowledge of who he is across the horizons. Light gives a simple image of this meaning. Light is called white light, and in itself it appears colorless.

White Light

![](images/86/a4e369cc-73b8-4b20-ad70-e25c3f6e278e.jpg)

When that light passes through a prism, something hidden becomes visible. A prism is a medium of glass. When white light strikes it, the light refracts; it bends. Because of that bending, a whole spectrum of colors appears, from violet to red and everything in between.

In the same way, the light of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى strikes creation. The Quran tells us that Allah is the light of the heavens and the earth:

> اللہ نور السموت والارض

Divine Light

![](images/86/c6127d05-d962-40d3-b020-fef39599aca6.jpg)

I understand that when the pure light of Allah's essence touches creation, and when it enters the physical realm, it refracts into the full spectrum of the divine attributes. This is why the universe contains so many different manifestations of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى's essence.

This عالم is the prism through which Allah's various attributes become visible in creation. Through it, I come to know Him in many ways:

- In one instance, creation shows that Allah is کریم, the generous.
- In another, the universe shows that He is جبار (the compeller).
- In another, the عالم reveals that He is قھار (the subduer).
- In another, He appears as عزیز (the powerful).
- In another, He is مُزِل (the humiliator).
- In another, He is معز (the bestower of honor).

So the عالم serves as a colossal theater for the divine manifestations of Allah's attributes, and through those manifestations I come to know Him.

I understand the arrangement in this way: the human intellect learns by defining and limiting things, just as the body works by breaking down food. For the intellect to digest knowledge, it must take in the whole, divide it into parts, and understand each part on its own. Only then can it gather those parts again and understand the whole.

The عالم presents these parts through the divine attributes. I reflect on each attribute individually, then bring them together to form a fuller understanding of my creator. This is an essential theme of Quran. It calls me to reflect on the heavens and the earth, the horizons and the souls, because through that reflection I recognize my creator and His abilities. Then it turns my attention inward, toward the soul, the نفس, so that I may understand the purpose of my own creation.

What, then, is the purpose of human creation?

### Soul: Purpose Of Creation

Scholars state that human beings were created for three reasons. This distinction is beautiful because it allows for purposeful existence not only for Muslims, but also for people of other beliefs, and even for people without belief.

The purposes of creation are these:

![](images/86/b55d6057-fdd9-410d-84b2-9b42960d764a.jpg)

The first, he says, is عمارۃ. Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى said:

> هُوَ أَنشَأَكُم مِّنَ ٱلْأَرْضِ وَٱسْتَعْمَرَكُمْ فِيهَا

We brought you forth from the earth to cultivate it.

The second reason, he says, is عبادۃ:

> وَمَا خَلَقْتُ ٱلْجِنَّ وَٱلْإِنسَ إِلَّا لِيَعْبُدُونِ

We only created the jinn, beings of the unseen realm, and human beings, beings of the visible realm, for عبادۃ, for willing worship. ابن عباس said in a narration that the purpose behind the creation of human beings was also لیعرفون: to know God. To know God is to love God. In the stages of love, عبودیۃ is one of the levels of محبۃ, of love. It is one of the higher levels of love. Therefore, He created the jinn and human beings to love Him.

The third and final reason for the creation of human beings is خلافۃ:

> وَيَسْتَخْلِفَكُمْ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ فَيَنظُرَ كَيْفَ تَعْمَلُونَ

We made you خلفاء because the خلیفۃ, the caliph, is the one who stands in someone else's place. He is the one who is left behind: ُخَلَفَہ. He remains in order to stand in the place of another. In other words, a خلیفۃ is a steward. Allah created us as His stewards on this earth.

Why? فَيَنظُرَ كَيْفَ تَعْمَلُونَ, so that He can witness what we do with all the outward and inward gifts He has blessed us with.

There are two types of خلافۃ. We have been made a caliph over ourselves, and then over others. For the خلافۃ of the self, Allah said:

> وَإِذْ قَالَ رَبُّكَ لِلْمَلَٓئِكَةِ إِنِّى جَاعِلٌۭ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ خَلِيفَةًۭ

Allah said to the angels, I have decided to create a caliph on the earth. In سُورَۃُ الصَّوَادِ He said to داود علیہ السلام . He said:

> يَدَاوُۥدُ إِنَّا جَعَلْنَكَ خَلِيفَةًۭ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ فَٱحْكُم بَيْنَ ٱلنَّاسِ بِٱلْحَقِّ وَلَا تَتَّبِعِ ٱلْهَوَىٰ فَيُضِلَّكَ عَن سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ ۚ إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ يَضِلُّونَ عَن سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ لَهُمْ عَذَابٌۭ شَدِيدٌۢ بِمَا نَسُوا۟ يَوْمَ ٱلْحِسَابِ

Oh داؤد! We have made you a caliph in the earth. This is the second kind of خلافۃ mentioned in the Quran.

There are two kinds of خلافۃ:

- ruling oneself;
- ruling others.

But the condition for ruling others is that one must first rule oneself. A person must first take control and mastery of his own نفس.

Here, داؤد is told that he has been placed in the earth to rule over others. He was made ruler over بَنِی اِسْرَائِیلَ, so that:

> فَٱحْكُم بَيْنَ ٱلنَّاسِ بِٱلْحَقِّ;

he may judge among them with the truth. Then He says:

> وَلَا تَتَّبِعِ ٱلْهَوَىٰ;

do not follow your caprice. In other words, one must rule oneself before ruling others. If that does not happen,

> فَيُضِلَّكَ عَن سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ,

the ف here is for causation. If a person follows caprice, whims, and appetites, they lead him away from the path of Allah.

So what are we as caliphs of Allah? How are we doing as Allah's representatives on earth? Allah says:

> سَنُرِيهِمْ آيَتِنَا فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ حَتَّىٰ يَتَبَيَّنَ لَهُمْ أَنَّهُ ٱلْحَقُّ ۗ أَوَلَمْ يَكْفِ بِرَبِّكَ أَنَّهُۥ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَىْءٍۢ شَهِيدٌ

We will show them Our Signs in the horizons and within our own souls. Allah makes the truth of who we are known through the signs around us and within us. Among those signs are the conditions visible in the horizons today, which reveal how we are doing as caliphs of Allah, just as a physician reads the symptoms of an ill patient. Allah says in سُورَۃُ الرُّومِ

> ظَهَرَ ٱلْفَسَادُ فِى ٱلْبَرِّ وَٱلْبَحْرِ بِمَا كَسَبَتْ أَيْدِى ٱلنَّاسِ لِيُذِيقَهُم بَعْضَ ٱلَّذِى عَمِلُوا۟ لَعَلَّهُمْ يَرْجِعُونَ

Mischief, corruption, and pollution have appeared on land and sea because of what human hands have earned. لِيُذِيقَهُم, the لام again gives the reason.

لِيُذِيقَهُم بَعْضَ ٱلَّذِى عَمِلُوا۟ لَعَلَّهُمْ يَرْجِعُونَ: so that Allah may give them a taste of some of what they have done, so that they may turn back. Allah allows our harmful actions to bring harmful consequences so that we may return to Him.

I follow the scholars who say that ‏ظَهَرَ ٱلْفَسَادُ is actually النقص فِى ٱلْبَرِّ وَٱلْبَحْرِ. It means that the blessings of the earth become fewer. The earth loses what once made it abundant.

It also means القحط: droughts, famines, and the harms that strike agriculture. The fruits of the land diminish. Lands that once grew crops stop producing. The benefits of the oceans also decrease: fish, whales, and the other creatures that live in the sea.

All of this happens because of what we earn through our sinfulness. The human being was created in the best of forms, in the very image of his creator, and with all his divine attributes. Yet when he turns away from his divinity and from his original state, he becomes capable of extreme corruption.

This was the very concern the angels expressed when Allah mentioned the creation of Adam to them. They said

> قَالُوٓا۟ أَتَجْعَلُ فِيهَا مَن يُفْسِدُ فِيهَا وَيَسْفِكُ ٱلدِّمَآءَ

Are you seriously considering creating a species therein who will create فساد and spill blood?

The word فساد carries many meanings in English: rottenness, corruption, decay, decomposition, putridity, depravity, wickedness, viciousness, iniquity, immorality, weakness, perversion, wrongness, and incorrectness. All these meanings are gathered inside فساد.

The English word pollution is defined as an undesirable condition of the natural environment, contaminated by harmful substances as a result of human activity.

بِمَا كَسَبَتْ أَيْدِى ٱلنَّاسِ: this is the result. Even in our own language, pollution names one of the dominant problems of the planet.

I am speaking of human responsibility for the present condition of the earth. The hardships that now surround us, including famine, extreme weather, earthquakes, and floods, are nothing other than بِمَا كَسَبَتْ أَيْدِى ٱلنَّاسِ: what our own hands have earned. Many countries have fallen into severe crisis because forests have been set on fire. Climate change raises temperatures, and higher temperatures bring more fires.

Another form of this فساد is in the air itself. In India, it has been estimated that half a million people die from air pollution. Fifteen thousand people died from terrorism, and the United States government moved heaven and earth to wage a war on terrorism. Yet half a million people suffer and die from air pollution. Where is the war against that?

Noise pollution is another part of the same corruption. In Pakistan and India, the levels are plain in the constant honking, yelling, and screaming, and in the noise of rickshaws and taxis with broken silencers. All of that is human-made فساد.

I know today that the world is moving quickly toward desertification. The reason is the loss of what is called topsoil. Desertification means that land once covered with growth becomes desert. This has already happened across large stretches of fertile land, and it continues to spread.

I was reading a book that said there was a time when all of North Africa was so fertile that, before بنو حِلاؤ entered Africa, a squirrel could run from سنجاب, from Egypt all the way to Morocco, without its feet ever touching the ground. The land was that fertile. But when the بنو حلاؤ came, they were like locusts. They devoured the resources completely. Desertification is therefore another example of بِمَا كَسَبَتْ أَيْدِى ٱلنَّاسِ: what our own hands have earned.

Another aspect of فساد appears in the words of Allah:

> وَٱلْأَرْضَ وَضَعَهَا لِلْأَنَامِ

The earth has been laid out for the انام. The word انام comes from نوم, which means sleep. أنام are creatures that sleep. This verse, then, concerns all sentient beings. It is not only about human beings; it includes the animal kingdom. Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى

> وَمَا مِن دَآبَّةٍۢ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ وَلَا طَٓئِرٍۢ يَطِيرُ بِجَنَاحَيْهِ إِلَّآ أُمَمٌ أَمْثَالُكُم ۚ مَّا فَرَّطْنَا فِى ٱلْكِتَبِ مِن شَىْءٍۢ ۚ ثُمَّ إِلَىٰ رَبِّهِمْ يُحْشَرُونَ
>
> There is not a moving creature on earth, nor a bird that flies with its two wings, but are communities like you. We have neglected nothing in the Book, then unto their Lord they shall be gathered.

I understand the verse to mean that no creature walks upon the earth, and no bird flies with its two wings, except that they belong to communities like human communities. Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى tells me that the kingdoms of animals are communities like ours. Then He says that He has left nothing out of this Book:

> مَّا فَرَّطْنَا فِى ٱلْكِتَبِ مِن شَىْءٍۢ

Even animals are acknowledged in the Quran. I know that some animals mate for life. I know that animals make immense journeys, pilgrimages, and migrations to distant places, much as human beings do.

Today, because of the sins of mankind, dogs are put on Prozac. In the United States, dogs are given Prozac, an antidepressant, because they are depressed in the homes of depressed and miserable people. Dogs are not naturally depressed animals. But so many houses in the United States are sick that the dogs and cats themselves become sick, because they are sentient beings. They are emotional beings. They feel and experience emotions as human beings do.

I see the problem in the endangered species brought about by human hands. Among the world's critically endangered species are some of the most beautiful and majestic animals. These are the very animals after which the ancient Arabs named their children: lions and tigers, أسد and فھد . They named their men after مُفْتَرِسَات, after animals that attack their prey. They named their women after the prey itself, such as ماھا and غزالۃ. So فھد goes after ماھا, and she does not stand a chance.

What strikes me is that the species endangered today are not the cockroaches. They are not the rats. They are not the snakes. These creatures are flourishing. The animals and species now endangered are the ones that represent divine qualities within the human being, qualities that are disappearing. The eagle, the peregrine, the lion, the tiger, and the elephant all stand for great human qualities that are vanishing.

> وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِكُمْ ۚ أَفَلَا تُبْصِرُونَ

Within the souls themselves, the call is to reflect. I see plenty of cockroaches in human disguise. I see plenty of rats and snakes in human disguise. But I no longer find the courage of lions or the majesty of eagles in human form. These qualities have become extinct among human beings.

I can go on and on about this, and I am sure much more could be added. But this is what we are doing as the caliphs and stewards of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. We are destroying the very earth that sustains us and gives us our livelihood. Then we complain that we have problems and ask why Allah does not solve them all.

Why should he? He is not our janitor. He is not our servant. He is not our errand boy. He created us in his image so that we could create جنۃ on earth, just as he created جنۃ in heaven. Adam was not created in جنۃ for amusement. He was created in جنۃ so that he could see and experience the beauty of that place. Then, when he came to the earth, he could cultivate the land and create that same جنۃ on earth.

> وَ أَنشَأَكُم مِّنَ ٱلْأَرْضِ وَٱسْتَعْمَرَكُمْ فِيهَا

He brought you forth from the earth to cultivate it.

That cultivation meant creating the same جنۃ on earth. What did we do instead? Why did this happen? How did things become so bad and slip so far out of hand? It happened because of our corruption: because of our فساد, our departure from the divine attributes and majestic qualities with which Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى created us. This is why he created the عالمین for us: so that we could study the signs on the horizons, the heavens and the earth, and learn from his divine attributes the ideal condition of the نفس, the ideal condition of the soul.

But we, as human beings, as خَلِیفَۃُ اللَّہِ, have failed terribly. We have shown that the angels had real grounds for concern. They knew clearly what they were saying when they said:

> أَتَجْعَلُ فِيهَا مَن يُفْسِدُ فِيهَا وَيَسْفِكُ ٱلدِّمَآءَ

Do you wish to create someone who will sow corruption on earth and spill blood? They knew clearly what they were saying.

So what have we turned away from? We have turned away from the divine attributes, from the character of Allah.

### AKHLAQ

Character

![](images/86/ce70c60c-96bc-46d3-9609-28618152e57f.jpg)

I begin with the word أخلاق, which comes from خُلق. This matters because we are called خَلق.

The word خَلق means creation. It is tied to the body, and the body is tied to the horizons. The word خُلق means character, and character belongs to the soul. Both words are made from the same letters, in the same order. In the science of Arabic, that shared form shows a close relationship in meaning.

خَلق belongs to the body, which is nourished by the physical world. خُلق is character, which takes its nourishment from the spiritual world. خَلق represents the body's existence; خُلق represents the soul's existence.

Both words begin with the letter خ (kha), a sound of friction in Arabic. It is a sound of effort. The body requires effort to develop, and character requires effort as well. Character demands great effort.

So if خَلق is the essence of the body, then خُلق, character, is the essence of the soul. When we die, the body returns to the earth with whatever it gained from the physical world. The soul returns to the spiritual world with the character it developed in this world. That is our true earning. That is our real acquisition: خُلق, character.

A soul that returns to its Master without developed خُلق is an impoverished soul. It has failed the purpose of its خلقۃ; it has failed the purpose of its creation. When the body is weak, it cannot benefit from what this physical world offers.

I know that this world offers many temporary pleasures. Yet if the body is extremely weak, and a person cannot even rise from bed, those pleasures cannot be enjoyed. In the same way, a soul that is empty and bankrupt of أخلاق cannot benefit from the pleasures of the afterlife.

How is character developed? Through revelation. The Quran, stated simply, explains the divine attributes of Allah. It shows what His attributes are and why they belong to Him. He is عظیم because he created this vast universe. He is قوی, all-powerful, because he did not merely create the universe; he created it with one single command: کُن, “Be,” and فیکون, it was.

In essence, the Quran describes Allah's attributes and explains why he is ال الہ. Our hearts witness these divine attributes in the light of revelation, and that witnessing creates **Belief**. The more the heart understands revelation, the more belief grows. That belief then nourishes and develops character.

Character, then, is nothing other than absorbing these divine attributes into the heart through revelation, and then reflecting those same divine attributes toward the rest of Allah's creation. That is character: the heart internalized, developed, and trained in the light of these divine attributes, then made visible in our dealings with Allah's creation.

### Interactions In Islam

That is أخلاق, and that is Islam. Islam is about interactions. It is معاملات. There are three types of interactions in Islam:

1. Interaction with Allah: حُقُوقُ اللَّہِ

1. Interaction with the self: حُقُوقُ النَّفْسِ

1. Our interaction with the creation of Allah: حُقُوقُ الْعِبَادِ

Huqooq Allah

![](images/86/d8bc7f29-420b-4720-94a6-45362c80bbb2.jpg)

Allah's right over us is that we recognize his attributes in the heavens and the earth, and that, through recognizing them, we come to love him for everything he has done for us. From that love, we willingly obey him and serve him as faithful slaves, as عبد. We worship him while knowing that only he deserves worship. That is the right of the Creator over his creation.

What, then, is the right of the self?

Huqooq Nafs

![](images/86/097a919e-1633-48a4-9509-d2ca569658fb.jpg)

The right of the self is that we reflect on the attributes of Allah in the عالم through the teachings of the Quran. We learn the attributes, qualities, and character of our master, then internalize and cultivate those qualities in our own hearts so that our character, our أخلاق, is formed.

We were created in the image of our master. We were created to represent our master on this earth. That means we must adopt the qualities and characteristics of our master. That is what a خلیفۃ is: a representative. Representation is not possible unless we become like the one we represent.

For instance, Allah is کریم. He is generous. Therefore, my soul has a right over me: I must train it in generosity.

The greatest generosity I can show my own soul is to protect it from sin and set it to work in good deeds. Allah is الحق. He is truthful. Therefore, my soul has a right over me: I must train it in truthfulness.

With whom must I be truthful? With myself.

The greatest falsehood in which the self is trapped is the delusion of دنیا. I must therefore train myself to wake from my delusions about this world. I must wake to the reality that I am here only for a short time. Everything I labor for in a worldly sense will be left behind. When I die, I will be left with nothing in this world except the title of میت. My loved ones, the people for whom I labor day and night, will be the first to place me in the grave and throw dirt over my face. I must not sacrifice my afterlife for this temporary life. That is truthfulness to the self.

The right of the self, then, is that I internalize the attributes of Allah and train myself in them, so that my character develops and I realize the potential with which I was created. I was created in the image of God, to act like God. And God is nothing but just.

This is why one of the divine names of Allah is المؤمن, which means, in essence, the giver of tranquility, the One who brings peace. It comes from the root امن, which means peace.

Huqooq Ibaad

![](images/86/951440aa-8e7c-46e7-a2f8-04c4aed6c38b.jpg)

Finally, I come to the right of others, حُقُوقُ الْعِبَادِ.

The right of Allah is to recognize his divine attributes in creation, and to submit to him willingly and lovingly in عبادۃ. The right of the self is to take those divine attributes into myself, so they shape my character and bring it into harmony with the character of Allah. I am to become like the Creator.

Only after that character has been formed does the final right, حُقُوقُ الْعِبَادِ, come into force. It means reflecting the divine attributes of Allah in my dealings with his creation.

I trained myself to be generous with my own self. Now that generosity must appear in my dealings with others. I trained myself to be truthful and sincere with my own self. Now that truthfulness, that صدق, that sincerity, must appear in my dealings with others. I recognized how Allah forgives me and how patient he is with me. Now I must reflect those attributes by being patient and forgiving toward others.

This is not an easy task. That is why the first two rights must be fulfilled before the third right can truly be fulfilled. A man once came to the Messenger of Allah and said:

> یَا رَسُولَ اللہِ. اَصِلُ اَخِیہِ وَ یَقطَعُنِی  وَ اُعطِیہِ وَ یَمنَعُنِی

“Oh messenger of Allah, I give to him, but he withholds from me. I come to him, but he cuts me off. What should I do?” the Prophet replied, “That is صِلَۃُ الرَّحِمِ. What you are doing is right. Continue meeting him, even though he cuts you off.”

That is Islam. And Islam is hard work. I must not think of this religion as easy. I must become one who strives. I must honor myself, because Allah honored me when he said:

> وَلَقَدْ كَرَّمْنَا بَنِىٓ آدَمَ وَحَمَلْنَهُمْ فِى ٱلْبَرِّ وَٱلْبَحْرِ
>
> “We have ennobled the children of Adam. We have made you leaders over the land and the oceans. We gave you a heart. We gave you an intellect. We nourish both with our revelation. We give you the ability to overcome the worst part of yourself. And even when you fall from time to time, We have given you the means to renew and restore your soul through توبۃ, through repentance.”

What a gift from Allah. I must do this with أخلاص, with sincerity, only for the sake of Allah. But it is not easy.

Three Dimensions of Islam

![](images/86/0f665992-7d40-40ca-833b-ab1b223125cd.jpg)

I understand these as the three dimensions of Islam: the rights of Allah, the rights of the self, and the rights of others. Recognizing the attributes of Allah in the heavens and the earth teaches the rights of Allah (حُقُوقُ اللَّہِ). Taking those divine attributes into myself, so that they shape my character, is the right of the soul (حُقُوقُ النَّفْسِ). Reflecting those divine attributes toward the creation of Allah is the right of others (حُقُوقُ الْعِبَاد).

The last of these is the hardest. It cannot be fulfilled unless the soul has first been lit by the character of Allah. This is why Rasul Allah ﷺ said in a hadees:

> أتخذو من أخلاق اللہ

Adopt the character and the qualities of Allah!

I find a narration in which Rasul Allah once saw a man speaking to Maulana Ali with great disrespect. Maulana Ali answered him with kindness. The man still continued in his harsh and disrespectful manner, yet Maulana Ali kept answering him with kindness again and again.

When the man left, the prophet asked him, یا علی! I saw this man speak to you with such disrespect and treat you so badly again and again. Yet each time, you showed him kindness and respect in return. Why was that?

Maulana Ali replied: یَا رَسُولَ اللہِ If this man is not willing to give up his wretched and evil nature in order to deal with me, then why should I give up my good character in order to deal with him?

SubhanAllah. What a beautiful statement. If this man is not willing to give up his wretched and evil nature in order to deal with me, then why should I give up my good character in order to deal with him? In other words, if this man insists on holding to something ugly, why should I let go of something beautiful that I possess? That is the beauty of أخلاق.

I therefore follow the example of Rasul Allah ﷺ, whom the Quran describes as reaching the pinnacle of اخلاق. Through his character, and through nothing but his character, he purified the most barbaric and vile people that had ever existed.

The history of Islam shows what kind of people he was sent to reform, and what mentality ruled them. They were worse than animals. Not a single virtue lived among them. They ate the flesh of elephants and crocodiles. When they ate a goat, they even boiled its hide and ate that too. They burned their enemies alive. They tore off their skin and wore it like a scarf. They cracked open a man's skull and used it as a cup for wine. Their women dyed their clothes in the blood of their enemies.

They did not merely kill the enemy. They tied his camel to his grave and left it there to starve to death. Sons married their mothers. Daughters were buried alive. They used the children of their enemies for target practice. Even their prayers were barbaric.

> یا ربِّ فَلا تَجعَل حَیَاتی مَذِلَّۃُ ولا مَوتَتِی بَینَ النِّسآءِ نوائِحِ

> ولاکن قَتیلاً تَمزَقُ الطَّیرِ حَولَہُ . و تَشرُبُ غِربانُ الدَّم من جوارح

Oh Lord, do not disgrace me in my life by letting me die on a bed between grieving women. Give me a death where birds tear out my flesh and crows drink the fresh blood gushing from my limbs.

I see the state of the Arabs when Rasul Allah came among them. This was the people he was sent to reform. He changed them into رَحْمَۃُ اللّٰہِ عَلَیْہِ. Their names are not mentioned without saying رَحْمَۃُ اللّٰہِ عَلَیْہِ- May the mercy of Allah be with them.

That is why, on the day of غدیر, Rasul Allah asked:

> اَلَسْتُ اَوْلَاکُمْ مِنْ اَنْفُسِکُمْ؟

Am I not more beloved to you than your own souls?

They replied:

> بلیٰ یَا رَسُولَ اللہِ

Indeed you are, O prophet of Allah.

> كُنَّا الْحَيَوَانَاتِ! كُنتُ جعلنا البشر

We were animals! You made us human beings.

How did he do this? Through his flawless اخلاق. He took the divine attributes of his creator into himself, formed through them a stainless character, and then reflected those same attributes back into creation with such force that the creator himself acknowledged:

> وَإِنَّكَ لَعَلَىٰ خُلُقٍ عَظِيمٍۢ

Indeed, beyond all doubt, You have reached the highest and most exalted level of character.

## The Womb Of Afterlife

أَعُوذُ بِاللهِ مِنَ الشَّيْطَانِ الرَّجِيمِ. ‏بِسْمِ ٱللَّهِ ٱلرَّحْمَنِ ٱلرَّحِيمِ ‎. اللَّهُمَّ صَلِّ عَلَى سَيِّدِنَا مُحَمَّدٍ وَعَلَى آلِ سَيِّدِنَا مُحَمَّدٍ كَمَا صَلَّيْتَ وَسَلَّمْتَ وَبَارَكْتَ وَرَحِمْتَ وَتَرَحَّمْتَ عَلَى إِبْرَاهِيمَ وَعَلَى آلِ إِبْرَاهِيمَ فِي الْعَالَمِينَ إِنَّكَ حَمِيدٌ مَجِيدٌ. رَبِّ اشْرَحْ لِي صَدْرِي وَيَسِّرْ لِي أَمْرِي وَاحْلُلْ عُقْدَةً مِنْ لِسَانِي يَفْقَهُوا قَوْلِي. السَّلَامُ عَلَيْكُمْ وَرَحْمَةُ اللَّهِ وَبَرَكَاتُهُ.

Now that we understand these meanings of رَبِّ and الرَّحْمَٰن and رَحِيم, now that we understand these terms, I want to share with you a beautiful spiritual representation that اللهُ سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى has created for us through the example of the womb, the رَحِيم. To quickly reiterate these terms, رَبِّ is a combination of six things in classical Arabic. The definition is a رَبُّهُ الْمَلِكُ وَالسَّيِّدُ الْمُرَبِّي الْمُرْشِدُ وَالْمُنْعِمُ الْقَيِّمُ. So a رَبِّ is an owner and he is the one with complete authority over its property. He is the one who nurtures his creation, guides it, benefits it by bestowing generous and undeserving gifts upon it, and he is the one who maintains it. So this is who اللَّه is, right? That's His primary role with His creation, of رَبّ. And all His other attributes, they basically center around and they stem from His رَبُّ الْبَرِيَّةِ. So if someone asks, who is اللَّه? The answer to that question is اللَّه is رَبّ. Now, as for His nature, His nature is الرَّحْمَٰن and الرَّحِيم. So the manner in which He executes, or the way He carries out the functions of His رَبُّ الْبَيَّة, it is through intense and consistent love, compassion and mercy.

So now that we understand these concepts, I want to share this example that اللَّه has created for us through the womb. You know, by studying the womb, we can actually understand a lot about our master's plan for us. Just by studying the relationship of the womb with its fetus. Because this womb, it is the first رُبَّ of the fetus. And it demonstrates the exact same principles of رَحْمَانِيَّة and رَحِيمِيَّة in its interaction with it. This is why there is a حَدِيثٌ in which رَسُولُ اللَّهِ, he said, this world is a womb for the afterlife. In other words, the story of our lives in this world, it tells a tale about the kind of life that we're going to have in the afterlife, because this world, this دُنْيَا that we're living in, is the womb, or acts as the womb, and we are the fetus in that womb. And just as the brief and temporary residence in a womb is actually preparation for this worldly life, our stay in this temporal world is nothing more than a short duration of preparation for the afterlife. Now, if you look at the رَحِيم, the womb, you'll see all six principles of رُبِّيَ at work. It has ownership and it has authority over the embryo, right?

It nurtures the embryo, it helps the embryo grow and develop into a fetus. So the womb is مَٰلِكِ, it is سَيِّد and it is مُرَبِّي. Then we see that the womb, it bestows these priceless gifts upon the fetus, to the development of its eyes, its nose, its ears, hands, feet, the heart, the intellect and all of its internal and external organs. So we can see that the womb is also the مُنْعِم, the bestower of gifts. We also know that the womb, it manages the entire development process of the embryo from its conception all the way until it is fully developed. So the womb is also قَيِّم. So we can see that the womb, it actually acts as the very first physical رُبَّ that we come in contact with. And this very first act, it actually demonstrates the design of how اللَّه created this mechanism. It demonstrates the act of رُبُوبِيَّة by carrying out all six functions of the definition of the word رَبِّ, right? مَلِكٌ، سَيِّدٌ، مُرَبٍّ، مُرْشِدٌ، مُهَيْمِنٌ، قَيِّمٌ. Now, what happens once the baby is delivered into this world?

Who is the baby handed over to? It is handed over to its loving mother, who becomes the next immediate رَبّ of that baby. Not the father, the mother. It's the mother that becomes the next رَبّ. Why? Because she feeds it. She takes care of it. There is this transference of رُبِّيَ that takes place. The mother now takes ownership of the baby. The mother now takes authority over it. The mother assumes the responsibility of nurturing it, helping it grow and develop, teaching it, guiding it, setting it to purpose, showering all these gifts and blessings upon it. She becomes the one who maintains that baby's sustenance in all its various forms, physical, emotional, psychological.

So we can see that our mothers become the next immediate رَبِّ. Right after the رُبُوبِيَّة of the womb, the رَحْمَٰن, it's completed. Now father is also a رَبِّ, but his رُبُوبِيَّة gets executed through the mother. You see, the father always uses the mother as a means for her. He facilitates her functions of رُبُوبِيَّة. That's the role of the father, but the primary caretaker of the child is always the mother.

So now let's study this حَدِيثٌ to better understand the underlying principles that are at work. So رَسُولُ اللَّهِ , he said, . This temporal world is the womb of the afterlife. So let's examine the fetus and the womb of its mother. Is it the womb actually doing all the work? Or is it the mother doing all the work behind the scenes and letting this womb act as an intermediary between herself and her child?

Is it the womb or the mother? We know it's always the mother, right? The mother is the one taking care of the baby's needs. She is the one eating all the right things. She is making sure she doesn't bump into things so that her baby doesn't get hurt. She's the one getting all the ultrasounds done, taking all those painful but necessary blood tests, all those injections. So she is the one actually suffering for the baby. Baby is living comfortably in the womb, but it's the mother doing all the work, right? The mother is the one doing all the hard work all along. But from the perspective of the fetus, who is doing all the work? The womb, not the mother. You see, the fetus has absolutely no knowledge of its mother.

All it knows, all it recognizes, all it understands is the womb. If I ask the fetus, who do you think is feeding you? Who do you think is nurturing you, taking care of you, sustaining you? Who is doing all the work that is necessary for your health and development? What will the fetus reply? The womb. The fetus has absolutely no knowledge of the existence of its mother. You see? What mother? Where's the mother? I don't see a mother. All I see is the womb.

There is no mother. It's just cause and effect. When I tell this fetus, no, no, it's not the womb, it's the mother. She is the one feeding you. She's the one taking care of you. This womb is only an intermediary. It's a means. And the fetus says, you know, what are you talking about? It's the umbilical cord that's feeding me. That's what gets me this blood that is carrying all the nutrients. I get fed by that, the umbilical cord. The umbilical cord is responsible for my development.
